#### **Technical Memorandum**

Date: February 27, 2020

To: Rick Demi, PE, Construction Manager

WSP/City of Modesto

From: John E. Juhrend, PE, CEG, Project Manager, Geocon

Subject: BCS Stockpiles Clean Cap Borrow Area Characterization Testing

State Route 132 Project, Modesto, California

This Technical Memorandum presents a summary of soil characterization testing performed at the proposed clean cap borrow area located adjacent and easterly of Carpenter Road within the State Route 132 right-of-way (see attached plan sheets). The proposed borrow area consists of relatively flat vacant land that was historically utilized for agricultural purposes prior to acquisition by the State in the 1960s. The proposed borrow area is approximately 1,250 feet long, 200 feet wide and will extend to an approximate excavation depth of 5 feet. The clean cap material will be utilized to cover consolidated barium containing soil (BCS) within existing Stockpiles 1 and 2 located easterly of the proposed borrow area. The soil sampling strategy utilized was developed in general accordance with the California Department of Toxic Substances Control (DTSC) *2001 Information Advisory Clean Imported Fill Soil* and was approved Mr. Dean Wright with DTSC.

On February 21, 2020 we completed 12 direct-push borings (B1 through B12) on a grid pattern within the borrow area as depicted on the attached plan sheets. The borings were performed to maximum depth of 5 feet. Continuous core soil samples were collected from each boring at depth intervals of 0 to 2 feet and 2 to 5 feet. The soil samples were transferred into new stainless steel tubes, placed in a chilled cooler, and transported to Advanced Technology Laboratories (ATL) under chain of custody protocol. ATL was instructed to composite the samples into depth discrete 3-part composites yielding four 3-part composite samples for each sampling depth interval. Each composite sample was analyzed for Title 22 metals following Environmental Protection Agency (EPA) Test Methods 6010B and 6461A (mercury). The four composite samples collected from a depth interval of 0 to 2 feet were further assigned for organochlorine pesticide (OCP) analysis following EPA Test Method 8081A.

Soil encountered in the borings generally consisted of brown to tan, damp, clayey to fine sandy silt. No field indicators of potential contamination impacts (i.e. staining, odors, debris, etc.) were observed during the field sampling activities.

The following presents a summary of reported concentration ranges (in milligrams per kilogram [mg/kg]) for Title 22 metals with site-specific background concentrations (see attached 2007 Shaw background data summary) in parentheses:

Arsenic 1.0 to 1.5 (1.2 mg/kg) Barium 60 to 110 (72.8 mg/kg) Beryllium 1.3 to 1.6 (0.2 mg/kg) Chromium 7.5 to 11 (7.1 mg/kg) Cobalt 3.2 to 4.9 (4.4 mg/kg) Copper 5.3 to 9.9 (7.5 mg/kg)

Image /page/229/Picture/13 description: The image contains the word 'GEOCON' written in a serif font with a teal-colored, metallic, beveled effect against a plain background. The text is centered horizontally.

Lead 1.7 to 7.0 (2.0 mg/kg) Nickel 5.6 to 8.2 (5.3 mg/kg) Silver 1.6 to 2.5 (0.2 mg/kg) Vanadium 16 to 25 (31.3 mg/kg) Zinc 21 to 41 (26.3 mg/kg)

Antimony, cadmium, selenium, thallium, mercury and OCPs were not detected in each soil sample analyzed above the laboratory reporting limits. A copy of the ATL laboratory report is attached.

The reported metal concentrations in the composite samples are consistent with naturally occurring background concentrations. Based on the data presented herein, the proposed clean cap borrow area appears to be suitable to generate clean cover fill materials for capping BCS Stockpiles 1 and 2. This information will be included in the Removal Action Completion Report to be submitted following completion of the stockpile capping activities.

NR Not Reported

#### **Technical Memorandum**

Date: Revised July 2, 2020

To: Rick Demi, PE, Construction Manager

WSP/City of Modesto

From: John E. Juhrend, PE, CEG, Project Manager, Geocon

Subject: Basin 5 Clean Cap Borrow Area Characterization Testing

State Route 132 Project, Modesto, California

This Technical Memorandum presents a summary of soil characterization testing performed at the proposed Basin 5 clean cap borrow area located adjacent and easterly of Carpenter Road within the State Route 132 right-of-way (see attached plan sheets). The proposed borrow area consists of relatively flat vacant land that was historically utilized for agricultural purposes prior to acquisition by the State in the 1960s. The proposed borrow area is approximately 750 feet long, 100 to 200 feet wide, and will extend to an approximate excavation depth of 10 feet. The clean cap material will be utilized to cover consolidated barium containing soil (BCS) within existing Stockpiles 1 and 2 located easterly of the proposed borrow area. The soil sampling strategy utilized was developed in general accordance with the California Department of Toxic Substances Control (DTSC) *2001 Information Advisory Clean Imported Fill Soil* and was approved Mr. Dean Wright with DTSC.

On June 16, 2020 we sampled 6 exploratory trenches (BASIN5 1 through 6) on a grid pattern within the borrow area as depicted on the attached plan sheets. The trenches were performed by the highway contractor to a maximum depth of 10 feet. Soil samples were collected from each trench at depth intervals of 0 to 2 feet, 2 to 5 feet, and 5 to 10 feet. We field composited the samples into depth discrete 3-part composites yielding two 3-part composite samples for each sampling depth interval. The soil samples were transferred into new stainless-steel tubes, placed in a chilled cooler, and transported to Advanced Technology Laboratories (ATL) under chain of custody protocol. Each composite sample was analyzed for Title 22 metals following Environmental Protection Agency (EPA) Test Methods 6010B and 7471A (mercury). The two composite samples collected from a depth interval of 0 to 2 feet were further assigned for organochlorine pesticide (OCP) analysis following EPA Test Method 8081A.

Native soil encountered in the trenches generally consisted of brown to tan, damp, clayey to fine sandy silt. No field indicators of potential contamination impacts (i.e. staining, odors, debris, etc.) were observed during the field sampling activities.

The following presents a summary of reported concentration ranges (in milligrams per kilogram [mg/kg]) for Title 22 metals with site-specific background concentrations (see attached 2007 Shaw background data summary) in parentheses:

Arsenic <1.0 to 1.8 (1.2 mg/kg) Barium 66 to 94 (72.8 mg/kg) Chromium 8.5 to 11 (8.6 mg/kg1 ) Cobalt 3.3 to 3.8 (4.4 mg/kg) Copper 7.5 to 9.3 (7.5 mg/kg) Lead 5.8 to 11 (2.0 mg/kg)

Image /page/266/Picture/13 description: The image contains the word 'GEOCON' written in a serif font. The letters are a metallic, dark teal-green color with a bevel effect that gives them a three-dimensional appearance against a plain white background.

Nickel 6.2 to 7.9 (5.3 mg/kg) Vanadium 33 to 37 (31.3 mg/kg) Zinc 29 to 36 (26.3 mg/kg)

Antimony, beryllium, cadmium, molybdenum, selenium, silver, thallium, mercury and OCPs were not detected in each composite soil sample analyzed above the laboratory reporting limits. A copy of the ATL laboratory report is attached.

The reported metal concentrations in the composite samples are consistent with naturally occurring background concentrations. Based on the data presented herein, the proposed Basin 5 clean cap borrow area appears to be suitable to generate clean cover fill materials for capping BCS Stockpiles 1 and 2. This information will be included in the Removal Action Completion Report to be submitted following completion of the stockpile capping activities.

<sup>1</sup> Based on 5-foot deep soil sample data

#### **Technical Memorandum**

Date: October 30, 2020

To: Scott Frenette, Construction Manager

WSP/City of Modesto

From: John E. Juhrend, PE, CEG, Project Manager, Geocon

Subject: Bent 2 Excavation Soil Stockpiles Characterization Testing

State Route 132 Project, Modesto, California

Reference: *Variance to Remedial Design Implementation Plan*, April 9, 2020

This Technical Memorandum presents a summary of Bent 2 excavation soil stockpiles characterization testing performed within the State Route 132 right-of-way. Approximately 4,000 cubic yards of native soil was excavated from Bent 2 (of the overcrossing structure under construction) located just westerly of State Route 99. The soil material was placed in two rows of end dumps extending approximately 800 feet along the southern portion of the top of Stockpile 2. A portion of the Bent 2 end dumps were placed on top of a fill berm generated from the lower portion of the Stockpile 2 mechanically stabilized earth (MSE) wall footing excavation and approved for use as clean cap by the California Department of Toxic Substances Control (DTSC). Photographs of the Bent 2 excavation and soil stockpile locations are attached.

We collected ten discrete soil samples from the Bent 2 end dump piles (STK1 through STK10) spaced at approximate 80-foot intervals (east to west) and alternating between end dump rows. The soil samples were placed in labelled Ziploc plastic bags and transported to California Laboratory Services (CLS) under chain-of-custody protocol. Each soil sample was analyzed for barium and lead following Environmental Protection Agency Test Method 6010B under expediated 24-hour turn-around time. A summary of the soil sample analytical data is on Table 1. A copy of the CLS laboratory report is attached.

Barium was detected in each of the 10 soil samples analyzed at concentrations ranging from 31 to 230 milligrams per kilogram (mg/kg). Four of the 10 soil samples contained barium at concentrations equal to or greater than the maximum site-specific background value for barium of 120 mg/kg. Lead was detected in 6 of 10 soil samples analyzed at concentrations ranging from 2.6 to 13 mg/kg, within similar range as the maximum site-specific background concentration for lead of 3.8 mg/kg.

Based on the barium data presented herein, the Bent 2 soil stockpiles do not meet the clean cap criteria as established in the referenced variance document as approved by DTSC (upperbound background threshold for barium of 120 mg/kg). However, pending DTSC review and approval consideration, the material may be suitable for use as partial clean cap (6 inches or less) on the upper surface of Stockpile 2 since additional clean cap consisting of the planned structural pavement section (base material and asphalt/concrete) will be placed over the entire upper surface of Stockpile 2. Fill material from previously approved clean fill sources will be required for use as placement of clean cap on the northerly slope of Stockpile 2.

This information will be included in the Removal Action Completion Report to be submitted following completion of the Stockpile 1 and 2 clean fill capping activities.

The design is comprised of multiple connected, angled segments with varying finishes; the left side is colored in a teal or dark greenish-blue, the top-right segments appear silver or gray, and there is a distinct, solid dark blue triangle pointing downwards in the center of the structure, partially enclosed by the other shapes. #### **Technical Memorandum**

Date: April 12, 2021

To: Rick Demi, Construction Manager

WSP/City of Modesto

From: John E. Juhrend, PE, CEG, Project Manager, Geocon

Subject: Carpenter Shoofly Clean Fill Characterization Testing

State Route 132 Project, Modesto, California

Reference: *Variance to Remedial Design Implementation Plan*, April 9, 2020

This Technical Memorandum presents a summary of the Carpenter Road Shoofly clean fill characterization testing performed within the State Route 132 right-of-way. Approximately 5,000 cubic yards of native soil will be excavated from the Carpenter Road Shoofly once the overcrossing structure under construction is completed. The contractor plans to use the excavated soil material as clean capping material to be placed over portions of barium containing soil (BCS) Stockpiles 1 and 2 pending California Department of Toxic Substances Control (DTSC) approval. Photographs of the Carpenter Road Shoofly are on the attached exhibit.

We collected 24 discrete soil samples from the Carpenter Road Shoofly along six vertical transects (CSF1 through CSF6) as depicted on the attached exhibit. Soil samples were collected from the surface of the soil material exposed on the slope face at 2, 5, 10 and 15 feet from the top of the slope. The soil samples were placed in labelled Ziploc plastic bags and transported to California Laboratory Services (CLS) under chain-of-custody protocol. The laboratory was instructed to composite three discrete soil samples from the same elevation along each slope face of the shoofly resulting in eight 3-part composite samples. The composite soil samples were analyzed for barium and lead following Environmental Protection Agency Test Method 6010B under expedited 48-hour turn-around time. A summary of the soil sample analytical data is on Table 1. A copy of the CLS laboratory report is attached.

Barium was detected in each of the 10 soil samples analyzed at concentrations ranging from 28 to 96 milligrams per kilogram (mg/kg), less than the maximum site-specific background value for barium of 120 mg/kg. Lead was detected in 9 of the 10 soil samples analyzed at concentrations ranging from 1.4 to 3.8 mg/kg, less than or equal to the maximum site-specific background concentration for lead of 3.8 mg/kg.

Based on the barium and lead data presented herein, soil material generated from excavation of the Carpenter Road Shoofly meet the BCS clean cap criteria as established in the referenced variance document as approved by DTSC (upperbound background threshold for barium of 120 mg/kg). The information presented herein will be included in the Removal Action Completion Report to be submitted following completion of the Stockpile 1 and 2 clean fill capping activities.

#### **Technical Memorandum**

Date: December 23, 2021

To: Dean Wright, PG, Project Manager

California Department of Toxic Substances Control

From: John E. Juhrend, PE, CEG, Project Manager

Geocon Consultants, Inc.

Subject: Draft Interim Removal Action Completion Report

Caltrans Modesto Soil Stockpiles

Phase 1 Interim State Route 132 West Project, Modesto, California

This Technical Memorandum has been prepared to present a progress summary of barium-containing soil (BCS) grading and clean fill capping operations completed to date for the Caltrans Modesto Soil Stockpiles located within Interim Phase I of the State Route 132 West Project in Modesto, California. The California Department of Toxic Substances Control (DTSC) is the lead regulatory agency providing site mitigation oversight under a Caltrans Interagency Agreement.

The attached incomplete Draft Interim Removal Action Completion Report (RACR) summarizes the current status of removal, consolidation and clean fill capping of BCS Stockpiles as highway embankment fill during Phase I construction as specified in the 2019 Remedial Design Implementation Plan. The BCS Stockpiles and aerially deposited lead impacted soil removed from the unpaved shoulder of northbound State Route 99 have been consolidated and capped with DTSC-approved clean fill materials. Remaining work includes additional placement of structural pavement section on the upper surface of the capped BCS Stockpiles, erosion control, drainage, and median barrier and signage.

A Final Interim RACR will be submitted following completed construction of Interim Phase I of the SR 132 West Project scheduled for opening to traffic in September 2022.

Meredith Williams, Ph.D., Director
8800 Cal Center Drive
Sacramento, California 95826-3200

#### Gavin Newsom

Governor

February 24, 2022

Mr. John E. Juhrend, PE, CEG Project Manager Geocon Consultants, Inc 3160 Gold Valley Drive, Suite 800 Rancho Cordova, California 95742 [juhrend@geoconinc.com](mailto:juhrend@geoconinc.com)

ACCEPTANCE OF THE DRAFT INTERIM REMOVAL ACTION COMPLETION REPORT – CALTRANS MODESTO SOIL STOCKPILES, PHASE 1 INTERIM STATE ROUTE 132 WEST PROJECT, MODESTO, STANISLAUS COUNTY, CALIFORNIA (SITE CODE: 900259)

#### Dear Mr. Juhrend:

The Department of Toxic Substances Control (DTSC) in consultation with the Central Valley Regional Water Quality Control Board (RWQCB) has reviewed the Draft Removal Action Completion Report – Caltrans Modesto Soil Stockpiles, Interim Phase 1 State Route 132 West Project (Draft Phase 1 RACR) submitted by Geocon Consultants Inc. (Geocon) on behalf of the Department of Transportation (Caltrans). The Draft Phase 1 RACR describes the consolidation and capping activities that have been completed to date for the barium containing soils (BCS) as part of the Interim Phase 1 Construction effort at the State Route 132 West project in Modesto, California (Site).

#### **Site Description**

Prior to the remediation activities, the Site consisted of BCS Stockpiles 1 through 3 within the Caltrans State Route 132 right-of-way in Modesto. Stockpile 1 was located approximately 150 feet westerly of Emerald Avenue and was roughly 600 feet long in the east-west direction, 160 feet wide, and had an estimated fill volume of 34,000 cubic yards (yd3). BCS Stockpile 2, located between Emerald Avenue and State Route 99, was roughly 1,650 feet long in the east-west direction, 160 feet wide, and had an estimated fill volume of 102,000 yd3. BCS Stockpile 3, formerly located on the east side

of State Route 99, was roughly 1,100 feet long, 120 feet wide, and had an estimated fill volume of 24,000 yd3.

#### **Site Remediation Activities – Soil Removal and Consolidation**

In accordance with the approved Remedial Action Plan (RAP) and approved Interim Phase 1 Remedial Design Implementation Plan (RDIP), the Draft Phase 1 RACR describes the work that was completed to excavate Stockpile 3, the western and eastern ends of Stockpile 2, and the removal of soil from the southern slopes of stockpile 1 and 2 to support the construction of the new State Route 132 Expressway. The removal of BCS Stockpile 3 included the excavation of approximately 27,675 yd3 of soil that was transported by truck and placed as embankment fill within the BCS Stockpile 1 containment zone. Twenty-nine confirmation soil samples were collected from the bottom of the BCS Stockpile 3 excavation area and analyzed for barium and lead. Three of the confirmation samples showed barium concentrations above the cleanup goal of 1,000 milligrams per kilogram (mg/kg) for this contaminant. These three areas were subsequently deepened by approximately one foot and resampled. The final confirmation samples showed that the remaining soil had concentrations below the cleanup goals for both barium and lead (80 mg/kg).

The removal of the western and eastern ends of BCS Stockpile 2 soil included the use of scrapers to remove the material from these areas and place it directly within the containment zone of BCS Stockpile 2 as embankment fill. Verification samples for barium and lead were collected from eight sample locations on the western end and 12 sample locations on the eastern end from native soil following removal of the contaminated material. Initial confirmation sample results indicated four areas above the 1,000 mg/kg cleanup goal for barium. These four areas were deepened by an additional foot and the resulting confirmation samples showed that all remaining soil in the western and eastern excavation areas were below the verification thresholds of 1,000 and 80 mg/kg for barium and lead, respectively.

Lastly, soil was removed from the southern slopes of BCS Stockpiles 1 and 2 to steepen them and allow for the construction of a mechanically stabilized earth (MSE) wall along this length of the new expressway. Prior to conducting this work, soil samples were collected at 200-foot intervals along the length of the southern slope of the two stockpiles at depth intervals of one foot, down to a maximum of five feet below the surface to determine if the material being removed could be used as clean cap soil. Results of the sampling indicated that all concentrations for barium and lead were below the previously mentioned cleanup goals for the two contaminants and lead was within background concentrations. However, barium was found above the site-specific

background concentration of 120 mg/kg, so all of the material removed was placed in the BCS Stockpile 1 and 2 containment zones as embankment fill using scrapers.

In addition to BCS soil removal efforts, soil with aerially deposited lead was removed from the unpaved shoulder of a portion of the existing State Route 99 adjacent to the location of BCS Stockpile 3 in accordance with the 'Soil Management Agreement for Aerially Deposited Lead-Contaminated Soils' between DTSC and Caltrans (DTSC, 2016). This material was placed in the containment zone in the eastern portion of BCS Stockpile 1.

#### **Site Remediation Activities – Clean Fill and Capping**

The containment zones for BCS Stockpiles 1 and 2 were capped with clean fill material identified near the project site. Four locations were identified as potential clean fill source areas and underwent sampling as per the "Information Advisory Clean Imported Fill Material" (DTSC, 2001). Three of the four areas were found to be acceptable sources based on the sampling results. Once in place, the capped material on the northerly slopes was vegetated with a hydroseed mix and an erosion control blanket was installed along with fiber rolls to minimize potential erosion. No such erosion control measures were necessary on the south side due to the construction of the MSE wall on that side of the new expressway.

#### **Acceptance of the Draft Removal Action Completion Report**

The Draft Phase 1 RACR indicates that the work completed to remediate the Site to date was accomplished as per the approved RDIP or the two variance requests to the RDIP approved on April 17, 2020 and September 21, 2020. DTSC is accepting the document as a technical status report with no comments.

As indicated in the document, As-Built Plan and cross section figures remain to be completed as does the final survey data and Operation and Maintenance (O&M) Plan for the Interim Phase 1 Construction Effort. These components will be included in the Final Phase 1 Removal Action Completion Report on the Caltrans Modesto Soil Stockpiles, Interim Phase 1 State Route 132 Project (Final Phase 1 RACR). The O&M Plan will be submitted for review such that an approved version can be incorporated into the Final Phase 1 RACR which is anticipated for submittal in July or August of 2022. The O&M Plan will require a soil management plan, an annual inspection of the pavement and other features of the containment remedy, as well as groundwater monitoring. As per DTSCs March 14, 2018 letter approving the RAP, following approval of the Final Phase 1 RACR, DTSC will enter into a O&M Agreement with Caltrans that will include the O&M Plan and complete a land use covenant for the containment areas.

However, certification of the RAP will not occur until after the Ultimate Phase 2 State Route 132 build-out effort is completed (Ultimate Phase 2).

The Ultimate Phase 2 effort is anticipated to begin in 8 to 10 years and will include adding additional lanes and completing the final build out of the State Route 132 project. This effort will include a supplemental RDIP for DTSCs review and approval as well as field oversight, an Ultimate Phase 2 RACR and revisions to the O&M Plan and O&M Agreement if necessary. The Ultimate Phase 2 State Route 132 project will be conducted under a separate agreement between DTSC and Caltrans prior to commencing the work.

DTSC and the RWQCB thank you for the efforts to ensure this site is safe for its intended use. Please contact me at (916) 255-3591 or via email at [Dean.Wright@dtsc.ca.gov](mailto:Dean.Wright@dtsc.ca.gov) if you have any questions.

Sincerely,

Dean Wright, PG Project Manager

Site Evaluation and Remediation Unit Site Mitigation and Restoration Program Department of Toxic Substances Control

Image /page/327/Picture/0 description: A logo featuring a stylized, abstract shape that resembles a repeating, interlocked pattern. The design consists of four interlocking segments arranged in a diamond or square orientation. The segments use a color palette of metallic-looking teal, black, and light grey or silver. In the center, there is a small, solid inverted blue triangle pointing downwards.

#### **Technical Memorandum**

Date: September 3, 2020

To: Rick Demi, Construction Manager

WSP/City of Modesto

From: David Watts, CAC, RDIP Field Monitor, Geocon Consultants, Inc.

John E. Juhrend, PE, CEG, Project Manager, Geocon Consultants, Inc.

Subject: Barium Containing Soil Air Monitoring Summary

State Route 132 Project, Modesto, California

*Remedial Design Implementation Plan, Interim Phase 1, Caltrans Modesto Stockpiles, State Route 132 West Freeway/Expressway Project, Stanislaus County, California*, Geocon Consultants, Inc.,

January 30, 2019

This Technical Memorandum presents a summary of air monitoring conducted during barium containing soil (BCS) grading operations completed during the Interim Phase I State Route (SR) 132 West Freeway/Expressway Project (the Project) in Modesto, California. The project location is depicted on the attached Site Plan, Figure 1. Three separate BCS stockpiles existed within Caltrans right-of-way (ROW) located south of the SR-99/Kansas Avenue interchange prior to the interim highway project grading activities. Stockpiles 1 and 2 are located west of SR-99 and Stockpile 3 was located east of SR-99.

#### **BACKGROUND**

Caltrans and the California Department of Toxic Substances Control (DTSC), in cooperation with the Central Valley Regional Water Quality Control Board, entered into an Interagency Agreement to address the BCS stockpiles. The stockpiles were placed in the early 1960s for the future SR-132 alignment and were partially generated from excavations in evaporation ponds from the former nearby FMC facility that contained elevated barium concentrations (primary contaminant of concern).

In accordance with the Interagency Agreement, Geocon Consultants, Inc. submitted the referenced *Remedial Design Implementation Plan* (RDIP) that describes the technical approach and provides detailed plans for mitigation of the BCS stockpiles. The DTSC-approved remedial alternative for the BCS stockpiles was containment by incorporating the stockpiles as highway embankment fill and covering with structural pavement section and imported clean fill during construction of the SR-132/SR-99 interchange portion of the Project.

Image /page/327/Picture/15 description: The image contains the word 'GEOCON' written in a serif font with a metallic, dark teal or green gradient and a 3D-effect shadow.

#### **AIR MONITORING ACTIVITIES**

In general accordance with procedures outlined in the referenced RDIP, Geocon performed air monitoring services between February and June 2020 during the following BCS grading activities:

- BCS mowing operations;
- Relocation of Stockpile 3 BCS material to Stockpile 2;
- Bridge abutment BCS excavations at east and west ends of Stockpile 2;
- Retaining wall BCS excavations along the southern sides of Stockpiles 1 and 2;
- Stockpiles 1 and 2 northerly slope grading;
- Placement of BCS material within Stockpiles 1 and 2 fill containment zones; and
- Drainage and other utility trenching within BCS Stockpiles 1 and 2.

With the exception of the planned use of BCS material temporarily placed on the upper surface of Stockpiles 1 and 2 for use as backfill material for the southerly mechanically stabilized earth retaining walls, BCS grading activities have been completed for the Project.

Perimeter air monitoring was performed during highway grading activities that involved significant disturbance of BCS stockpile material (surface preparation, excavation, loading, transport, fill placement, etc.). We provided ambient perimeter air monitoring during the BCS stockpile grading activities to document total airborne particulate and metals (i.e., barium and lead) concentrations in accordance with protocols presented in the referenced RDIP. Results of the air monitoring were used to assess the effectiveness of the contractor's dust control measures. Air monitoring tasks included:

- Documenting and photographing the locations of air monitoring stations;
- Monitoring daily meteorological forecasts to anticipate onsite wind direction and speed;
- Collection of personal, upwind and downwind air samples over a minimum sampling period of 4.5 hours for analysis of barium, lead and total dust; and
- Verifying that downwind direct-read, real-time particulate counter readings (pDR-1200 monitors) did not exceed the preset Fence Line Action Level for total dust of 4.0 milligrams per cubic meter (mg/m<sup>3</sup> ).

Real-time MIE pDR-1200 direct reading instrument measurements were compared to the Fence Line Action Level of 4.0 milligrams per cubic meter (mg/m3 ) to confirm that "wet methods" used during BCS stockpile grading activities were effective in controlling offsite migration of total airborne dust. Real-time monitors were calibrated according to the manufacturer's instructions and programmed to log total dust levels every 60 seconds. In addition, audible alarms on monitors located downwind of excavation, soil handling, and sampling activities was set to the highest "Total Dust" level recorded on ambient upwind monitors.

Personal monitoring conducted during the BCS grading activities (required by project specifications) included representative sampling of equipment operators (open cab mower, compactor operator, etc.). Perimeter monitoring conducted during the BCS grading activities included one representative upwind (background) sample and at least two representative downwind (offsite) sampling locations. Air monitoring was performed using real-time direct reading particulate counters with PM-10 inlets and active sampling attachments for 37-millimeter (mm) filter cassettes using personal sampling pumps calibrated at 2.0 to 3.0 liters per minute. Match-weighed 0.8-micron mixed cellulose ester (0.8µ MCE) filters were analyzed under expedited turn-around-times by LA Testing for total dust following NIOSH 0500 and barium and lead in accordance with NIOSH 7300 modified.

Field direct-read and laboratory total dust data was compared to the RDIP Fence Line Action Level of 4.0 mg/m³. Barium results were compared to an action level of 25 micrograms per cubic meter (µg/ m³) as presented in the highway contractors' *Health & Safety Plan* prepared by Adanta, Inc., November 2019. Lead results were compared to an action level of 1.5 ug/µm³ as presented by the California Air Resources Board (CARB) 30-day average ambient lead public health standard. Tabular summaries of laboratory and real-time air monitoring data and LA Testing laboratory reports are attached.

#### SUMMARY

The results of air monitoring performed during BCS grading activities for the Project indicate airborne levels of total dust, barium, and lead below designated project action and risk levels, confirming effective dust control measures.

This information will be included in the Removal Action Completion Report to be submitted following completion of the Stockpile 1 and 2 clean fill capping activities.

Attachments: Site Plan

332007371

### 1. Identify the relationship between the quantities

- The price of a gallon of milk is directly proportional to the number of gallons purchased.

### 2. Set up the proportion

$$\frac{\text{Price}_{1}}{\text{Gallons}_{1}} = \frac{\text{Price}_{2}}{\text{Gallons}_{2}}$$

### 3. Plug in the known values

$$\frac{\$3.65}{1 \text{ gallon}} = \frac{x}{5 \text{ gallons}}$$

### 4. Solve for x

$$x = 3.65 \times 5$$

$$x = 18.25$$

### Conclusion

The total price for 5 gallons of milk is **\$18.25**.

Subject: OPERATIONS AND MAINTENANCE PLAN

CALTRANS MODESTO SOIL STOCKPILES

INTERIM PHASE 1 STATE ROUTE 132 WEST PROJECT MODESTO, STANISLAUS COUNTY, CALIFORNIA

#### Mr. Wright:

In accordance with the Interagency Agreement between the California Department of Toxic Substances Control (DTSC) and the California Department of Transportation (Caltrans), we are pleased to submit this Operations and Maintenance Plan (O&M Plan) for the Caltrans Modesto Soil Stockpiles (the Site). The Site is located along the eastern portion of the alignment of the newly constructed State Route (SR) 132 West Project in Modesto, Stanislaus County, California.

This O&M Plan has been prepared for barium-containing soil stockpiles that were consolidated, capped with clean fill material within designated Stockpile 1 and 2 Containment Zones, and utilized as highway embankment fill between State Route 99 and west of Emerald Avenue during construction of Interim Phase I of the SR 132 West Project. Comments provided by the DTSC in writing dated August 1, 2022 on the Draft O&M Plan dated March 2022 have been addressed in this O&M Plan.

Please contact us should you have any questions regarding this O&M Plan.

# **OPERATIONS AND MAINTENANCE PLAN CALTRANS MODESTO SOIL STOCKPILES SR 132 WEST PROJECT - INTERIM PHASE 1**

## **1.0 INTRODUCTION**

<span id="page-476-0"></span>This Operations and Maintenance Plan (O&M Plan) has been prepared by Geocon Consultants, Inc. on behalf of the California Department of Transportation (Caltrans) for the Caltrans Modesto Soil Stockpiles (the Site). The Site is located along the eastern portion of the alignment of the newly constructed State Route (SR) 132 West Project in Modesto, Stanislaus County, California (Project Location Map, Figure 1). The California Department of Toxic Substances Control (DTSC), in cooperation with the Central Valley Regional Water Quality Control Board (CVRWQCB), is the lead regulatory agency providing site mitigation oversight under a Caltrans Interagency Agreement.

This O&M Plan has been prepared for barium-containing soil (BCS) Stockpiles 1 through 3 that were consolidated, capped with clean fill material, and utilized as highway embankment fill during Interim Phase 1 construction of the SR 132 West Project in accordance with the 2019 *Remedial Design Implementation Plan* (RDIP) and Caltrans-approved project design plans and specifications. The RDIP provided the technical approach and detailed plans for interim implementation of the selected remedial remedy identified in the 2018 *Final Remedial Action Plan* (RAP). The selected remedial alternative to mitigate exposure to BCS was consolidation and containment by incorporating the stockpiles as highway embankment fill and covering with clean fill and structural pavement section during Interim Phase 1 and Ultimate Phase 2 construction of the SR 132 West Project.

Interim Phase 1 of the SR 132 West Project included the construction of two of four planned traffic lanes from east of SR 99 to North Dakota Avenue and related highway bridge structures and interchanges. BCS Stockpiles 1 through 3, portions of which contain elevated levels of barium and lead, have been contained during Interim Phase 1 of the project by utilizing them as clean capped embankment material within the overall limits of BCS Stockpiles 1 and 2 located adjacent and extending westerly of SR 99 to beyond Emerald Avenue (Preconstruction Site Plan, Figure 2). SR 132 was opened to traffic on November 3, 2022. Ultimate Phase 2 construction will include highway widening to four traffic lanes.

<span id="page-476-1"></span>

### **1.1 Site History and Background**

From the 1930s to 1970s, property beneath and northeast of the SR 99/Kansas Avenue Interchange was occupied by chemical processing facilities operated by Food Machinery and Chemical Corporation (FMC) and its predecessors. Ores and minerals including barite (barium sulfate) and celestite (strontium sulfate) were processed for use in greases, lubricating oil and pigment blanks. Sodium sulfide was generated as a by-product and sold as a caustic and reagent. From the 1950s to the 1970s, liquid residue ("tailings") generated at this facility was discharged to unlined evaporation ponds. In 1961, the State of California purchased a 4.3-acre parcel in the southwestern portion of the FMC facility, including a portion of the ponds, for the construction of the SR 99 freeway through Modesto. Pond tailings and underlying soils from the FMC property along with native soils excavated south of the SR 99/Kansas Avenue interchange were subsequently placed to create BCS Stockpiles 1 through 3.

Prior to construction of Interim Phase 1 of the SR 132 West Project, the Site consisted of BCS Stockpiles 1 through 3 within Caltrans SR 132 right-of-way (ROW) east and west of SR 99. The BCS Stockpile material is primarily comprised of layered, poorly graded sand and silty sand similar to underlying native alluvial deposits of the Modesto Formation. The average maximum stockpile fill thickness was approximately 20 feet with a total estimated volume of 160,000 cubic yards.

The potential for the BCS Stockpiles to contain residual chemicals associated with the former FMC impoundments was identified in a 2003 *Initial Site Assessment* performed during the highway project development process. Caltrans subsequently entered into an interagency agreement with DTSC, with cooperation of the CVRWQCB, for assessment and mitigation regulatory oversight.

<span id="page-477-0"></span>

### **1.2 Environmental Investigations and Remedial Actions**

Several environmental investigations were conducted at the Site by Caltrans including the following major technical studies:

- Site investigations including stockpile characterization, groundwater monitoring well installation, and stormwater sampling performed between 2004 and 2006.
- Preliminary Endangerment Assessment including human health risk assessment (HHRA) completed in 2009.
- Additional Stockpile 3 characterization completed in 2012.
- Supplemental site investigation including stockpile perimeter and fence line sampling and updated HHRA completed in 2013.
- Groundwater and stormwater monitoring reporting since 2012.
- Feasibility Study (FS) presenting evaluation of remedial alternatives completed in 2014.
- RAP presenting the selected remedial alternative completed in 2018.
- RDIP completed in 2019 presenting the technical details of implementing the RAP during Interim Phase 1 construction of the SR 132 West Project.

Completed remedial actions at the Site include partial removal of Stockpile 3 in 2012 to facilitate an offramp widening project and consolidation, and containment and clean capping of Stockpiles 1 through 3 between 2020 and 2022 during construction of Interim Phase 1 of the SR 132 West Project. Stockpile 3 BCS material was entirely removed from the east side of SR 99 and placed within the Stockpile 1 Containment Zone. A summary of the completed BCS stockpile remedial actions at the Site is presented in the 2022 *Removal Action Completion Report* (RACR).

Copies of the environmental investigation and monitoring reports for the Stockpile 1 and 2 BCS Containment Zones at the SR 132 West Project are accessible from the following regulatory websites:

https://www.envirostor.dtsc.ca.gov/public/profile\_report.asp?global\_id=60001626 https://geotracker.waterboards.ca.gov/profile\_report.asp?global\_id=SL0609924194

<span id="page-478-0"></span>

### **1.3 BCS Containment Zones (Restricted Areas)**

BCS at the Site has been consolidated and clean capped within the designated Stockpile 1 and 2 Containment Zones (highway embankment fills) during construction of Interim Phase 1 of the SR 132 West Project. The Stockpile 1 Containment Zone is located adjacent and west of Emerald Avenue between approximate SR 132 Stations 1125+50 and 1133+50. The Stockpile 2 Containment Zone is located between Emerald Avenue and SR 99 between approximate SR 132 Stations 1135+35 and 1148+30.

The BCS material contains elevated barium concentrations (up to 72,000 milligrams per kilogram that exceeds regulatory screening levels) and to a lesser extent elevated lead concentrations. Aeriallydeposited lead (ADL) impacted soil generated from the northbound shoulder of SR 99 containing lead concentrations exceeding California hazardous waste thresholds was further placed within the Stockpile 1 BCS Containment Zone.

As-built plans depicting the aerial extent of the Stockpile 1 and 2 BCS Containment Zones are presented on Figures 3-1 and 3-2. Typical cross sections of the Stockpile 1 and 2 BCS Containment Zones are presented on Figures 4-1 through 4-4. Profiles of the Stockpile 1 and 2 BCS Containment Zones are presented on Figures 5-1 and 5-2. Photographs of the completed SR 132 highway improvements, and a legal description and boundary survey of the Stockpile 1 and 2 BCS Containment Zones are attached.

## **2.0 SOIL MANAGEMENT PROCEDURES**

<span id="page-479-0"></span>This section describes procedures to be implemented should BCS material within the Stockpile 1 and 2 Containment Zones (Restricted Areas) be disturbed. Those involved with disturbance of BCS material within the Stockpile 1 and 2 Containment Zones are responsible for implementing the procedures in this O&M Plan.

Caltrans general highway maintenance procedures may be accessed from the below website:

https://dot.ca.gov/programs/maintenance/maintenance-manual

Where requirements presented herein conflict with standard Caltrans maintenance procedures, the requirements presented within this O&M Plan shall take precedence.

<span id="page-479-1"></span>

### **2.1 Notification of BCS Disturbance**

Any disturbance of BCS material within the Stockpile 1 and 2 Containment Zones must be performed in compliance with this O&M Plan. Caltrans is responsible for minimum ten working day advance notification to DTSC of any planned activities that may disturb BCS material and/or associated clean cover fill materials. No BCS disturbance is permitted without prior written authorization from DTSC.

<span id="page-479-2"></span>

### **2.2 Health and Safety**

Workers involved with and in the vicinity of BCS material disturbance/handling shall implement reasonable health and safety precautions and wear appropriate personal protective equipment. Workers shall attend onsite health and safety briefings to communicate the potential human health risks associated with exposure to elevated barium and lead concentrations in soil. At a minimum, onsite worker health and safety measures should include the following:

- Using gloves and wearing long sleeve/long pant clothing to minimize dermal exposure to BCS material.
- Washing face and hands before eating, drinking, or smoking to minimize potential ingestion of BCS material.
- Decontaminating excavation equipment and tools in contact with BCS material.

Contractors/workers involved with disturbance of BCS material shall be required to comply with all applicable California Occupation Safety and Health Administration rules and regulations.

<span id="page-480-0"></span>

### **2.3 Dust Mitigation**

Proper dust mitigation measures shall be implemented during any BCS material disturbance activities to minimize fugitive dust emissions in accordance with San Joaquin Valley Air Pollution Control District applicable rules. Water spray application should be sufficient to maintain proper dust control during BCS material handling in addition to covering of temporary stockpiles and truck loads. Work should be suspended during high winds or where dust control measures are inadequate to control generation of visible dust.

<span id="page-480-1"></span>

### **2.4 BCS Material Stockpiling**

Excavated BCS material requiring temporary storage (less than 30 days) prior to onsite replacement or offsite landfill disposal shall be placed in a designated onsite and secured stockpile area. The premoistened BCS material shall be placed on and covered with minimum 6-millimeter-thick plastic sheeting surrounded by erosion control measures (i.e. straw wattles, hay bales, etc.) in accordance with California Health and Safety Code 25123.3(b)(4)(A). The stockpile cover shall be secured with ropes and weights (i.e. sand bags, tires, etc.).

<span id="page-480-2"></span>

### **2.5 BCS Material Reuse/Offsite Disposal**

Removed BCS material may be placed back within the Containment Zone excavation from where it was generated from provided the BCS material is properly compacted and covered with minimum 12-inchthick DTSC-approved clean fill material and/or structural pavement section. The proposed source(s) of clean fill material shall be properly evaluated based on DTSC's 2001 *Information Advisory Clean Imported Fill Material*.

Excess excavated BCS material requiring offsite disposal will require stockpile sampling and characterization testing for California-licensed landfill acceptance. The required sampling frequency and laboratory analysis is determined by the landfill facility. At a minimum the BCS stockpile characterization samples shall be analyzed for Title 22 metals following Environmental Protection Agency Test Methods 6010B and 7471A (mercury). Soluble metals testing using the Waste Extraction Test and Toxicity Characteristic Leaching Procedure may be required for samples with elevated total metal concentrations to determine if the BCS material is a California or Federal Resource Conservation and Recovery Act hazardous waste.

An authorized Caltrans representative shall sign any required non-hazardous waste and/or hazardous waste manifests as the generator. The designated transporter of the BCS material to the accepting landfill facility shall maintain appropriate licensing.

<span id="page-481-0"></span>

### **2.6 Stormwater Management**

Caltrans is responsible for maintaining stormwater best management practices to minimize uncontrolled runoff and potential discharge of pollutants. Caltrans further performs period stormwater sampling in the vicinity of the Stockpile 1 and 2 BCS Containment Zones during significant precipitation events. The BCS material is buried beneath highway pavement and clean fill material along the northern fill slopes of the Stockpile 1 and 2 Containment Zones and therefore is not in direct contact with stormwater runoff.

<span id="page-481-1"></span>

### **2.7 Site Access and Security**

The Site is a portion of an active State highway with no non-motorized public access. The perimeter of the SR-132 ROW in the vicinity of the Stockpile 1 and 2 BCS Containment Zones has perimeter fencing further restricting public access. Caltrans will periodically patrol the Stockpile 1 and 2 BCS Containment Zones (Restricted Areas) to confirm no active trespassing or other unauthorized access (i.e. homeless encampments) that may have impacted the clean cap. Any breaches to the perimeter security fencing and resulting clean cap damage will be documented on an O&M Site Inspection Form as described in Section 3.1. Any related security fencing and/or clean cap damage shall be repaired in a timely fashion.

## **3.0 OPERATIONS AND MAINTENANCE PROCEDURES**

<span id="page-482-0"></span>This section summarizes the O&M procedures that will be implemented by Caltrans to ensure the integrity of the clean capped Stockpile 1 and 2 BCS Containment Zones. The O&M activities will be completed pursuant to the O&M Agreement entered into between DTSC and Caltrans.

<span id="page-482-1"></span>

### **3.1 Inspections**

Caltrans will perform minimum annual inspections during the third quarter of each year (July through September) prior to the rainy season. During the annual site inspections, the Stockpile 1 and 2 BCS Containment Zones (Restricted Areas) will be observed for any visual indicators of damage to the cap. The annual inspections will include the following:

- Observations of Stockpile 1 and 2 BCS Containment Zone surface cover including mechanical stabilized earth retaining walls along the southern boundary, highway pavement, and northerly clean capped fill slopes.
- Observations of drainage swales, storm drain inlets and slope down drains, and other stormwater conveyance structures.
- Observations of perimeter security fencing and indicators of unauthorized access and trespassing.
- Documentation of any significant pavement cracks/distress, erosion, or other indicators of compromised clean cover materials.
- Documentation of required maintenance.

In addition to the annual inspections, supplemental inspections will also occur after significant events such as fires, substantial rainfall (1 inch in 24 hours), seismic activity, traffic accidents that may damage the clean cap, homeless encampment cleanups, etc.

The field observations will be documented on the attached O&M Site Inspection Form during the annual and supplemental site inspections. The findings of the inspections will further be documented in an annual report submitted to DTSC as discussed in Section 4.3.

<span id="page-482-2"></span>

### **3.2 Maintenance and Repairs**

Maintenance and repairs will be performed on an as-needed basis based on the findings of the annual inspection. Required maintenance will be completed within 30 days of the site inspection unless an extension is approved by DTSC. Typical routine maintenance and repair activities may include, but are not limited to the following:

- Pavement pothole repairs and crack sealing
- Repair of stormwater runoff and erosion damage
- Repair of slope failures
- Fencing repairs

## **4.0 REPORTING**

<span id="page-483-0"></span>This section summarizes reporting requirements for any Stockpile 1 and 2 BCS Containment Zone disturbance activities and reporting of O&M activities. Notification and documentation regarding implementation of this O&M Plan shall be between the following Caltrans and DTSC contacts:

Richard Stewart, PG, Engineering Geologist Caltrans Central Region Environmental Division Hazardous Waste & Paleontology Branch 2015 E. Shields Avenue, Suite 100 Fresno, California 93726 559.383.5568, richard.c.stewart@dot.ca.gov

Dean Wright, PG, Project Manager California Department of Toxic Substances Control Site Mitigation and Restoration Program 8800 Cal Center Drive Sacramento, California 95826 916.255.3591, Dean.Wright@dtsc.ca.gov

An addendum to this O&M Plan will be prepared in the event of a change in contact for Caltrans and/or DTSC.

<span id="page-483-1"></span>

### **4.1 Notification of BCS Disturbance Activities**

DTSC requires advance notification of any planned work within the Stockpile 1 and 2 BCS Containment Zones that may expose and/or disturb BCS material. The BCS work notifications shall include the following information:

- The scope and purpose of the planned work
- Estimated start and completions dates
- Type and depth of BCS disturbance
- Estimated volume of BCS material to be stockpiled
- Final destination of stockpiled BCS material (reuse within Containment Zone and/or landfill disposal)
- Site plan depicting work areas

The Caltrans designated contact will coordinate with DTSC prior to initiation of the work. No BCS disturbance activities will be permitted without prior written DTSC authorization.

<span id="page-484-0"></span>

### **4.2 Documentation for BCS Disturbance Activities**

Within 30 days of completing work within the Stockpile 1 and 2 Containment Zones that disturbs BCS material, Caltrans shall provide written documentation of the activities to DTSC. The written documentation shall include the following information:

- A description of the completed BCS disturbance activities
- Locations and depth of BCS disturbance
- Volume of BCS material removed from Containment Zones
- Volume of BCS material stockpiled for offsite landfill disposal
- Site plan depicting BCS disturbance areas
- Copies of BCS material characterization data (laboratory reports and chain-of-custody documentation), copies of landfill waste disposal manifests and tonnage receipts, and site photographs

<span id="page-484-1"></span>

### **4.3 O&M Annual Inspection Summary Report**

An annual report will be prepared by Caltrans to summarize any BCS material disturbance activities, and to document the findings of the annual inspection and any required associated maintenance activities. The annual report will be submitted to DTSC by January 31st for the prior calendar year.

The annual report will include the following elements:

- A summary of completed BCS disturbance activities
- A summary of the annual site inspections and any periodic inspections
- A summary of any completed maintenance actions and/or repairs
- Details of any significant changes to site conditions
- Copies of the O&M Site Inspection forms
- Photographs depicting site conditions and any areas requiring maintenance and/or repairs
- Conclusions regarding the ongoing effectiveness of the clean cap/containment system

The annual reports will document the findings of the site inspections and confirm that the clean cover materials over the Stockpile 1 and 2 BCS Containment Zones are effective in preventing direct exposure to contaminated soil. The annual reports will further confirm that any required repairs and/or maintenance activities have been completed, and that the Site remains compliant with the DTSC-required Land Use Covenant.

<span id="page-485-0"></span>

### **4.4 Five-Year Review**

Caltrans will perform five-year reviews to assess the effectiveness of the interim Stockpile 1 and 2 BCS containment measures. The five-year reviews will focus on monitoring and maintenance of the implemented containment measures and potential for exposure to contained and capped BCS/ADL soil materials. Monitoring of groundwater and stormwater are routinely reported to DTSC and the CVRWQCB until permanently suspended and therefore would not be a focus of the reviews. DTSC will provide project certification following completion of the final containment measures implemented during Ultimate Phase 2 of the SR 132 West Project.

Five-Year Review Reports will include the following elements:

- Introduction
- Site background
- Physical setting
- Site chronology
- Removal actions
- Five-year review process
- Technical assessment
- Issues
- Conclusions
- Recommendations
- Cost impacts

The "Technical Assessment" section will include a summary of the previous four Annual O&M Inspection Summary Reports. The Five-Year Review Reports will also contain photographs depicting site conditions.

Further to the right, a chain-link fence divides the pathway from the side of a large, light-colored industrial-style building. Utility poles and power lines extend along the path. The sky is clear and blue, indicating a bright, sunny day.

A portion of the South one-half of Section 30, Township 3 South, Range 9 East, Mount Diablo Meridian, County of Stanislaus, State of California, described as follows:

Image /page/501/Figure/0 description: A technical survey plat titled 'EXHIBIT B STOCKPILE AREAS' dated November 8, 2022, prepared by Northstar Engineering Group, Inc. The drawing illustrates two distinct rectangular land survey areas labeled 'STOCKPILE-EAST' (5.83 ACRES) and 'STOCKPILE-WEST' (3.45 ACRES), separated by Emerald Avenue. The site is bounded by various streets including Kansas Avenue and Elm Avenue, with State Route No. 99 shown to the left. The document includes detailed land boundary measurements, bearings, a 'POINT OF COMMENCEMENT' mark (2 inch iron pipe), and 'TRUE POINT OF BEGINNING' (T.P.O.B.) notations for each stockpile area. The plat is officially stamped and signed by a Professional Land Surveyor, Rien Groenewoud (No. 6946), for a portion of the South one-half of Section 30, T.3 S., R.9 E., M.D.M.
